I’d have to think about it.\n\nDoctor: During that wired period, were you hospitalized because of how you were acting or feeling?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that… I don’t think I was hospitalized, no. And now lately it’s the opposite — I’m so slowed down my sister says it takes me forever just to get my shoes on.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you were pacing all night and felt unable to stop, did you hear or see things that other people couldn’t?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I wasn’t hearing voices or seeing things, at least not that I noticed. I was just really wired and couldn’t settle down.\n\nDoctor: During that wired period, did you feel unusually powerful or important?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. I really thought I was onto something huge with this bilingual supper club idea. Like, I honestly believed it was going to be the best one in the city, and that investors would be dumb not to put money into it — even though I didn’t actually have a job or any money lined up.\n\nDoctor: During that wired time, did you feel unusually happy or euphoric?\n\nPatient: Yeah. After I got fired, I felt… weirdly unstoppable, like excited in a way that didn’t really fit what was happening. But it also wasn’t just happy — if anyone questioned my plans or told me to slow down, I’d get really angry, like they were trying to ruin my life.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve had low times, but I don’t know if it was most of the day almost every day like that. I’d have to think about it.\n\nDoctor: During that wired period, were you hospitalized because of how you were acting or feeling?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that… I don’t think I was hospitalized, no. At least, not that I remember.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices when you were not feeling especially high, energized, or depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not really sure what would count as hearing voices, but I haven’t noticed that happening when I wasn’t already in some kind of mood state.\n\nDoctor: During that wired period, did your behavior cause serious problems in your life, like getting into major trouble at work, with money, or with other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It caused a lot of problems. I couldn’t keep work plans straight, and I was messing up stuff with rent and things I’d promised my family I’d do. It got to the point where my sister had to take me to a crisis clinic because I just couldn’t get basic responsibilities organized on my own.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s been more than two weeks — honestly, the heavy, slowed-down feeling has been going on for about three months now, most days.\n\nDoctor: During these past three months, have you been feeling sad or down most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it sad the whole time. It’s more like feeling down and heavy most days, yeah, but I don’t know if it’s every day or all day.\n\nDoctor: During these past three months, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It’s been more than two weeks — honestly, the heavy, slowed-down feeling has been going on for about three months now, most days.\n\nDoctor: During these past three months, have you been feeling sad or down most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it sad the whole time. It’s more like feeling down and heavy most days, yeah, but I don’t know if it’s every day or all day.\n\nDoctor: During these past three months, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Things I usually like—cooking, taking food pictures, listening to music, playing pickup soccer, even volunteering—just feel kind of flat now. I know I used to care about them, but I can’t really make myself feel interested.\n\nDoctor: During these past three months, have you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “depressed” most of the day nearly every day. I’ve been feeling really down and heavy a lot, but it’s hard for me to pin down exactly how much of the day or how many days.\n\nDoctor: During these past three months, have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that.
